```
def smooth_objective_gradient(t_vars, n, j0, beta):
    t = np.empty(n); t[j0]=0; t[others]=t_vars
    # stable softmax
    m = np.max(t)
    e = np.exp(t-m)
    a = e / e.sum()
    c = fast_convolve(a)
    # optionally clip tiny negative
    maxc = c.max()
    # logsumexp
    beta_c = beta*c
    beta_m = beta*maxc
    # underflow: exp(beta_c - beta_m)
    p = np.exp(beta_c - beta_m)
    p_sum = p.sum()
    # if p_sum inf? beta_c<=beta_m so exp<=1; p_sum finite.
    F = maxc + np.log(p_sum) / beta
    # weights p = exp(...)/p_sum
    p /= p_sum
    # d = cross_corr(a,p_rev)
    # reverse p
    pr = p[::-1]
    conv_len = n + (2*n-1) -1 =3n-2
    d = linear_convolve(a, pr)[:3n-2]
    L=2n-1
    d = d[L-1::-1]  # d_i for i=0..n-1? d[L-1-i], so reversed slice length n.
    Q = np.dot(a*a, d)
    grad_full = 2*a*(a*d - Q)
    return F, grad_full
```
Check d slicing: linear_convolve length at least n+L-1. conv[k]=∑ a_j pr[k-j]. We want k=L-1-i? Then convex? We derived d_i=conv[L-1-i]. So d array [d_0...d_{n-1}] = conv[L-1 - np.arange(n)] = conv[L-1::-1]. Good. Need ensure conv length >= L-1+n-1? L-1 =2n-2, min index n-1; okay.

But `linear_convolve(a, pr)` with rfft returns length maybe pad; we can cut. For n=2048, 3n-2 ~6142, fft_size 8192.

Gradient scale: F ~0.0007, grad? ~a^2 d - aQ. d ~ weighted a values ~ maybe 0.001, a^2~1e-5, product ~1e-8. L-BFGS can handle. Maybe multiply gradient by 2*n? Since score=2nF, objective scale irrelevant. Could minimize score directly: objective = 2nF, grad=2n grad. This makes numbers ~0.001? Actually 2n*grad maybe 0.01? Either fine. Algorithm "dual ascent / best response multiplicative":
- Normalize a.
- Compute c=a*a, M=max c.
- Choose weights p (softmax or top active) over conv peaks.
- Compute d_i=∑ p_k a_{k-i}.
- b = d / sum(d) (ensure positive). This b minimizes max conv(a,b)? Approximately.
- Update a = (1-t)a + t b (or a←b). Recompute.
This is analogous to fictitious play experience weighting but using dual weights from own peaks. Could be very efficient and improve global peaks. It directly attacks peak lags: if a peak at k, it increases coordinates i,j pair? d_i =∑ p_k a_{k-i}. For a peak k, d_i weights a_{k-i}, the conjugate part. Then b proportional to partner densities. We can include a `dual_best_response` phase:
```
def dual_iters(a0, time_limit, beta=..., top_k=..., verbose):
  a normalized; conv; M; best
  for it in while time:
    p = softmax(beta*c) or top-k weights
    d = cross_corr(a,p[::-1])
    b = np.maximum(d,0); b/=b.sum()
    # line search alpha in [0.1,0.3,0.5,0.8,1]
    for alpha: cand=(1-alpha)*a+alpha*b; normalize; score=2n fast_convolve(cand).max(); if < best update
    a = best_cand
    if no improve reduce beta/top? break after many
```

def run_lgbfgs(a0, time_limit, beta_list=(50,200,1000,5000,20000,100000), maxfun_per_stage=300, verbose=False):
    n=len(a0)
    a = normalize(a0)
    # choose j0 with value near median log
    pos = np.clip(a,1e-300, None)
    logs = np.log(pos)
    med = np.median(logs)
    j0 = int(np.argmin(np.abs(logs-med)))
    # initial t
    t_full = logs - logs[j0]
    t_full = np.clip(t_full, -30, 30)
    t_full[j0]=0
    # args for minimize
    from scipy.optimize import minimize
    best_a = a.copy(); best_score = score_norm(a)
    for beta in beta_list:
        if time.runondic... > limit: break
        # determine maxfun maybe based on remaining and beta
        rem = limit - elapsed
        maxfun = int(min(1000, max(100, rem*2)))? Evaluations per second unknown.
        # Use smaller maxfun later? 
        fun_wr? Need top-level function with args cannot capture beta dynamic? We can pass args=(n,j0,beta). But `minimize` needs a function. We have top-level `smooth_score_grad(t_vars, n, j0, beta)`.
        res = minimize(smooth_score_grad, t_vars, args=(n,j0,beta), method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, bounds=bounds, options={'maxiter':200,'maxfun':maxfun,'ftol':1e-14,'gtol':1e-12})
        # recover a if res.x finite
        a_new = recover softmax(res.x,n,j0)
        score = eval_norm(a_new)
        if score<best_score: best...
        # warm start t from res.x for next (already)
    return best_a, best_score
```

Implement `cross_corr_lag(a,p)` top-level:
```
def cross_corr_lag(a,p):
    n=len(a); L=len(p); total=n+L-1
    fs=1 << (total-1).bit_length()
    A=rfft(a,fs); P=rfft(p[::-1],fs)
    conv=irfft(A*P,fs)[:total]
    return conv[L-1::-1]
```
But p[::-1] creates reversed p. L=2n-1. `conv[L-1::-1]` length n? If conv length total=3n-2, start index 2n-2 down to 0 inclusive length 2n-1? Wait we need length n. Let's re-derive indices carefully!

We want d_i for i=0..n-1 =∑_{j=0}^{n-1} a_j p_{i+j}, with p length L=2n-1 (i+j ranges i to i+n-1 ≤ i+n-1 ≤2n-2 for i≤n-1). Good.
Define pr[m] = p[L-1-m], length L.
conv = a * pr. conv[k]=∑_{j=0}^{n-1} a_j pr[k-j] if 0≤k-j<L.
Want ∑ a_j p[i+j] = ∑ a_j pr[L-1-(i+j)] = ∑ a_j pr[(L-1-i) - j]. So k = L-1 - i. Thus d_i = conv[L-1 - i]. For i=0 -> index L-1=2n-2. For i=n-1 -> index L-1-(n-1)=n-1. So slice conv[L-1 : n-2 : -1]? Actually start=2n-2, stop=n-2 (exclusive), step -1 gives indices 2n-2 down to n-1 inclusive length n. In Python `conv[L-1 : n-2 : -1]` (stop n-2). If we use `conv[::-1]` would include too many. Previous `conv[L-1::-1]` from 2n-2 down to 0 length 2n-1, not n. Need correct: `conv[L-1 : n-2 : -1]`. Let's test n=2, p len3, want d0=p0a0+p1a1? k=2. d1=p1a0+p2a1? k=1. `conv[2:0:-1]` gives [conv2,conv1] length2. Good.

But if n=1, stop? handle.

`total=n+L-1`, `conv` length total. Start index L-1 within. Stop n-2 could be negative if n=1. Use:
```
if n==1: return conv[:1]
return conv[L-1 : n-2 : -1]
```

def generate_bump(n, f_factor, bump_amp, width):
  f=forward_unnorm(n)
  # reduce leading? f *= f_factor?
  x=np.arange(n)
  bump = np.exp(-(n-1-x)/width)
  a = f_factor*f + bump_amp*bump
  a=np.maximum(a,0); a/=a.sum()
```

Dual best response:
```
def dual_best_response(a0, time_limit, beta=200, top_k=20, max_iters=..., verbose=False):
    n=len(a0); a=normalize(a0); best=a; best_score=score_norm(a)
    alpha_list = [1.0,0.7,0.5,0.3,0.2,0.1]
    no_improve=0
    while time...:
       c=fast_convolve(a); M=c.max()
       # weights: softmax beta c or top_k average
       p=softmax_weights(c, beta)
       d=cross_corr_lag(a,p)
       b=d/np.maximum(d.sum(),1e-300)
       # line search alpha
       best_local=None; best_local_score=inf
       for alpha in alpha_list:
           cand=(1-alpha)*a+alpha*b; cand/=cand.sum()
           sc=score_norm(cand)
           if sc<best_local_score: ...
       if best_local_score < best_score - tol:
           a=best_local; best_score=...; no_improve=0
       else:
           no_improve+=1
           # reduce beta? maybe increase beta or try top weights
           if no_improve>20: break
    return best,best_score
```
This uses exact score each alpha (6 FFTs per iteration). Could be many. Maybe use fewer alpha [0.5,1.0] and compute score for best. Use p=softmax. Could be less effective. We can make optional short time.

L-BFGS likely stronger; dual maybe not needed. But dual cheap can provide diverse starts.

Coordinate descent:
We need final exact polish. Implement `coordinate_descent(a0, time_limit, step_factors=None)`:
- Normalize a, compute conv, M, S=1? We'll allow unnormalized.
- Use current a unnormalized. S=a.sum(); score=2nM/S^2.
- `t0`; while time:
   improved=False
   order = np.arange(n) maybe random or reverse. If random, need top-level arg? Use rng? In helper can use global? We can pass rng or use np.random.
   For i in order:
      if a[i]<=1e-14: continue
      k0=i; k1=min(i+n-1,2n-2)
      # unaff current
      left = conv[:k0].max() if k0>0 else -1
      right = conv[k1+1:].max() if k1+1<L else -1
      unaff=max(left,right)
      base = conv[k0:k1+1]
      a_shift = a  # length n; for k-i from0..n-1
      old=a[i]
      best_factor=1.0; best_newM=M; best_score=S...
      for f in step_factors:
         y=old*f; if y<0 continue
         delta=y-old
         # new sum
         S_new = S + delta
         if S_new <1e-8: continue
         # affected values
         affected = base + 2*delta*a_shift
         idx2 = 2*i - k0
         if 0 <= idx2 < n:
             affected = affected.copy()? if we only modify copy; affected is new array from base +..., so okay.
             affected[idx2] = conv[2*i] + 2*old*delta + delta*delta
         # Unaff may be stale? We computed from current conv for this coordinate before update, and no update yet. Exact.
         newM = max(unaff, float(affected.max()))
         score = 2*n*newM/(S_new*S_new)
         if score < best_score - 1e-15:
            best_score=score; best_newM=newM; best_factor=f; best_y=y
      if best_factor !=1:
         delta=best_y-old
         # update conv slice
         changes=2*delta*a_shift
         idx2=2*i-k0
         if 0<=idx2<n:
             changes[idx2]=2*old*delta+delta*delta
         conv[k0:k1+1] += changes
         a[i]=best_y; S+=delta; M=best_newM? But true M may be max(unaff, updated slice best_newM). We used newM exact for this update based on current unaff. Good.
         improved=True
         # update global M; best_newM exact (unaff + new affected). However unaff from current conv, which includes all latest updates. exact. So M=best_newM.
         # If cur score < best_score_overall update best? Keep best_a.
   if not improved: break
   # optional normalize after sweep
```
This is O(n^2) per sweep with NumPy affected length n per factor. To reduce memory, `affected = base + 2*delta*a_shift` allocates each factor. Newton for p*p = target u (constant). Given p, residual r = u - p*p. We want Δ such that 2p*Δ + Δ*Δ ≈ r. For small Δ, solve least squares JΔ=r, with JΔ=2p*Δ. Since p nonnegative, J is Toeplitz. We can solve in Fourier: Δ_hat = R_hat/(2 P_hat) (regularized), then inverse, clip, line search. This is like deconvolution to target. Because target constant, R_hat is known (Dirichlet). This may converge to a p whose square is flat. The formal square root of uniform full support not exact but least squares maybe. Since p must have length n and nonnegative, projection. Detailed Newton implementation:
```
def newton_flat(p0, time_limit, target_mode='uniform', eps=0.05, verbose=False):
    n=len(p0); p=norm(p0); L=2n-1
    N=1 << (L-1).bit_length()
    P_fft = rfft(p,N) updated after p changes.
    u = np.full(L,1.0/L)
    best=p; best_score=score
    lam = 1e-5
    no_improve=0
    alphas = [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05]
    while time...:
       c=fast_convolve(p)
       sc=2n*c.max(); update best
       if target_mode=='uniform': target=u
       else:
          M=c.max()
          newM=max(M*(1-eps), 0.02*sc? ) # reduce 5%
          target=np.minimum(c,newM).copy(); target/=target.sum()
       r = target - c
       R=rfft(r,N)
       # slope: derivative p*p = p*Δ + Δ*p? For real, two terms? Wait derivative of p*p wrt Δ is p*Δ + Δ*p = 2 p*Δ (linear convolution) only if p and Δ commutative? Convolution commutative, p*Δ = Δ*p; derivative of p*(p+εΔ) = p*Δ + p*Δ? Actually p*p coefficient ∑ p_i p_{k-i}; derivative wrt p_j =2 p_{k-j}; linear op is 2 p*Δ. So yes.
       d_hat = (2*np.conj(P_fft)*R) / (4*np.abs(P_fft)**2 + lam)
       d=irfft(d_hat,N)[:n]
       # If target mode shave, maybe p sum changes; line search normalize.
       found=False
       for alpha in alphas:
          cand=p + alpha*d
          # ensure nonneg
          if np.any(cand < -1e-12): continue? We clip anyway.
          cand=np.maximum(cand,0.0)
          s=cand.sum()
          if s<1e-12: continue
          cand/=s
          scand=score_norm(cand)
          if scand < sc - 1e-14: # or < sc?
              p=cand; found=True; no_improve=0; break
       if not found:
          # try smaller alpha random? or adjust lam
          no_improve +=1
          lam = max(lam*0.5,1e-12) if no_improve <10 else min(lam*2,1e-2)
          if no_improve > 50: break
       # optionally update P_fft after p
       P_fft = rfft(p,N)
       if target_mode=='shadow': eps*=0.98? 
    return best
```
The derivative for `p*p` when p changes by Δ: (p+Δ)*(p+Δ)-p*p = p*Δ + Δ*p + Δ*Δ = 2p*Δ + Δ*Δ. Since convolution commutative. Good.

But Least squares normal equation in time: J^T J ≈ 4 P^T P? In FFT with zero padding, using |P|^2 is approximate. Could instead compute a small step by direct gradient descent on residual? Newton may overshoot. But line search handles.

However N=2n-1, p and Δ length n; circular conv of length N equals linear conv if Δ length ≤N-n+1? Actually for linear conv of lengths n,n, need N≥2n-1. The convolution theorem with zero padding and circular conv gives exactly linear conv padded. The normal equation for minimizing ||PΔ - R||_2 over Δ in frequency is diagonal if the operator is circular convolution with P. But our operator as circular conv of length N with p and Δ (Δ length N? We are allowing Δ full length N, then taking first n). If we let Δ length N (with zeros beyond n?) Then we take Δ_full and use its first n? But if Δ_full has nonzero beyond n, ignoring loses. We can instead allow update of p by taking first n of Δ_full; the ignored tail corresponds to extending p? Alternative solve linear system J^T J Δ = J^T r where J is L x n Toeplitz (2 p shifted). J^T J is n x n Hankel. Could use conjugate gradient with J action via FFT and J^T action via cross_corr. We want solve A Δ = b where A=J^T J (n x n), b=J^T r.
J Δ = 2 p*Δ (length L). J^T v = 2 cross_corr(p, v?) Specifically derivative: We need (J^T v)_i = ∑_k 2 a? For J acting on Δ: (JΔ)_k = 2∑_j p_j Δ_{k-j}. Then J^T v_i =2∑_k v_k p_{k-i} = 2 cross_corr(p, v)_i. Good.
So b_i=2 cross_corr(p, r)_i.

Use CG for Δ? A is symmetric? J^TJ yes PSD. But not positive definite if p has zeros? Regularize. We can solve A Δ = b with CG 20 iterations. Initial Δ=0. This gives Gauss-Newton step. Complexity 20*FFT per outer, maybe 2000 FFT; okay.

    # b = Jt(r)
    b = 2*cross_corr(p, r)  # length n? cross_corr returns length n for p len n and r length L? For v length L, d_i=2∑ v_k p_{k-i}. Use cross_corr_lag(p? Actually p is a, v is r. def newton_shave(p0, time_limit):
  p=norm; c; M=max; best...
  target_level = M*0.9
  while time:
     # target based on level
     target = np.minimum(c, target_level)
     target /= target.sum()
     d=... target - c
     line search; if new M < target_level: # success, keep p, reduce target_level = max(mex? newM*0.98) ...
     else: # fail, increase target_level toward old M? 
```

Implement:
```
def coordinate_descent(a0, time_limit, tol=0.02, verbose=False):
  a=norm; conv=fast; M=max; S=1; best...
  sweep=0
  while time:
    # active coords
    active_mask = np.zeros(n,bool)
    thresh = M*(1 - tol) + 1e-300
    active_lags = np.where(conv >= thresh)[0]
    if len(active_lags)==0: active_lags=[int(np.argmax(conv))]
    for k in active_lags:
       i0=max(0,k-n+1); i1=min(n-1,k)
       active_mask[i0:i1+1]=True
    coords = np.nonzero(active_mask)[0]
    if len(coords)==0: coords=np.arange(n)
    improved=False
    for i in coords:
       ... as before
    # after sweep, if no improved and tol<0.5: tol*=2 else break
    # recompute score and best, renormalize
```
But if active_mask only top peaks, coordinate changes may create new peaks later; full CD will catch next sweep.

  p=norm; c; M; best; level=M

    target = np.minimum(c, level)
    target[target<0]=0; target/=target.sum()
    ... Newton step to target

Let's implement a generic `newton_target(p, target, ...)` and wrapper. But top-level functions no closures.

Pseudo:
```
def newton_to_target(p0, target, time_limit, reg=1e-6, alphas=(1,0.5,0.25,0.1), max_no_improve=30):
    n=len(p0); p=norm(p0); L=2*n-1; N=nextpow2(L)
    best=p; best_score=score_norm(p)
    c=fast_convolve(p); sc=...
    t = np.asarray(target); t = t.copy(); t=np.maximum(t,0); s=t.sum(); if s>0: t=t/s
    no=0
    P = rfft(p,N)
    while time_left:
       # compute d
       r = t - c
       R = rfft(r,N)
       denom = 4*np.abs(P)**2 + reg
       d_hat = (2*np.conj(P)*R)/denom
       d = irfft(d_hat,N)
       # Since d may have energy outside first n? Use d[:n]. To preserve sum maybe adjust? We normalize cand.
       step = d[:n]
       # optionally remove mean? Not needed because normalize removes scale.
       found=False
       for alpha in alphas:
           cand = p + alpha*step
           cand = np.maximum(cand, 0)
           s=cand.sum(); if s<1e-15: continue
           cand=cand/s
           scand=score_norm(cand)
           if scand < best_score - 1e-14:
               p=cand; best=p.copy(); best_score=scand; c=fast_convolve(p); found=True; no=0; break
       if not found:
           no+=1
           # adjust reg
           if no %5==0:
              reg = max(reg*0.5,1e-12) if no<20 else min(reg*2,1e-2)
           if no>max_no_improve: break
       P = rfft(p,N)
    return best,best_score
```
Need target in normalized scale (sum1). For uniform, target = ones/L. `score_norm` returns 2n max conv / sum²; since candidate sum1, 2n max.

Potential issue: The step `d` from frequency least squares may not be real? irfft returns real. Good.

def score_and_conv(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)

```
def smooth_score_grad(v, n, j0, beta):
    t = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    t[j0] = 0.0
    m = np.max(t)
    exp = np.exp(t-m)
    a = exp / exp.sum()
    c = fast_convolve(a)
    # stable logsumexp
    if beta <= 0:
       p = np.full(len(c), 1/len(c))
       soft = float(c.sum())? Wait smooth max with beta=0 is mean, not max. We want as beta->0? Use beta positive.
    maxc = float(c.max())
    z = beta*(c - maxc)
    z = np.clip(z, -745.0, 0.0)
    ez = np.exp(z)
    s = ez.sum()
    if s <= 0 or not finite: ...
    p = ez/s
    soft = maxc + np.log(s)/beta
    d = cross_corr_lag(a, p)
    # P = sum a*d? For softmax gradient we need P = sum_k p_k c_k (not soft exactly). Since smooth F = soft, derivative wrt c =p. ∑ p c = P.
    P = float(np.dot(p, c))
    gradF = 2.0 * a * (d - P)
    score = 2.0 * n * soft
    grad = 2.0 * n * gradF
    grad[j0]=0
    return float(score), grad
```

We don't need soft in P? Yes P. If β→∞ and p one peak, P=c_k. For n=2 center uniform, d=0.5, P=0.5 grad0. With finite β endpoints in p, P larger/smaller? Good.

```
def run_lgbfgs(a0, time_limit, betas=(10.0,50.0,200.0,1000.0,5000.0), maxfun_per_beta=800, verbose=False):
    n=len(a0); pos=...; j0=...
    t0 = np.log(np.maximum(a0,1e-300)); t0-=t0[j0]; t0=np.clip(t0,-35,35); t0[j0]=0
    bounds=[(-35,35)]*n; bounds[j0]=(0,0)
    best=a0; best_score=score_norm(a0)
    t= t0.copy()
    for beta in betas:
       rem=time_limit elapsed; if rem<10 break
       maxfun=int(min(2000, max(100, rem*5))) # heuristic
       res=minimize(smooth_score_grad, t, args=(n,j0,beta), method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, bounds=bounds, options={'maxfun':maxfun,'maxiter':200,'ftol':1e-14,'gtol':1e-10})
       if res.x finite:
          a_new=recover_softmax(res.x,n,j0)
          sc, = score_and_conv(a_new)
          if sc<best_score: best=a_new; best_score=sc
          t=res.x.copy(); t[j0]=0
       # If no improvement and beta high? continue with smaller? Actually betas increasing.
    return best,best_score
```
But if `minimize` at β=10 from asymmetric may move; then β=50 warm start. Good.

# final verify evaluate_sequence
```

def dual_improve(a0, time_limit, beta=100):
  a=norm; c; score; best...
  no=0; beta_cur=beta
  while time:
    c=fast_convolve(a); score=...
    p=softmax_weights(c, beta_cur)
    d=cross_corr_lag(a,p)
    b=d/sum(d)
    found=False
    for alpha...
       cand=(1-alpha)*a+alpha*b; normalize; score; if <score accept
    if accept: no=0 else:
       no+=1
       if no%10==0: beta_cur*=1.5; if beta_cur>10000: beta_cur=10; 
       if no>50: break
  return best
```

Detailed helper implementations:

```
import numpy as np
import time
from scipy.fft import rfft, irfft

def _next_pow2(m): return 1 << (int(m)-1).bit_length()
```
If m=1, (0).bit_length=0 ->1. Good.

`fast_convolve`:
```
def fast_convolve(a):
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0: return np.array([])
    size = 2*n -1
    fs = _next_pow2(size)
    A = rfft(a, fs)
    c = irfft(A*A, fs)[:size]
    # remove tiny imaginary? irfft real
    c[np.abs(c) < 1e-18] = 0.0
    return c
```
`irfft` with A*A complex; okay.

`score_and_conv`:
```
def score_and_conv(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    s = a.sum()
    if s < 1e-14: return np.inf, None
    a = a / s
    n = len(a)
    c = fast_convolve(a)
    c = np.clip(c, 0.0, None)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(c)): return np.inf, None
    return float(2*n*c.max()), c
```

`normalize_seq`.

`make_forward`:
```
def make_forward(n):
    a=np.empty(n,float); a[0]=1.0
    for i in range(1,n):
       a[i]=a[i-1]*(2*i-1)/(2*i)
    return a/a.sum()
```
For n large, values not underflow.

`make_bump`:
```
def make_bump(n, factor=0.3, width_frac=0.15):

   f=np.empty(n); f[0]=1; recurrence.
   x=np.arange(n)
   width=max(1,n*width_frac)
   bump=np.exp(-(n-1-x)/width)
   a = f + factor * bump
   a=np.maximum(a,0); return a/a.sum()
```

`interpolate_seq` from previous:
```
def interpolate_seq(f, new_n):
  old_n=len(f); f=clip; s=sum; if s<... return uniform
  f=f/s
  old_x=np.linspace(0,1,old_n); new_x=np.linspace(0,1,new_n)
  out=np.interp(new_x, old_x, f)
  out=np.maximum(out,0); ss=out.sum(); if ss>0: out/=ss
  return out
```
Could use periodic? okay.

`softmax_weights`.

`cross_corr_lag` as above. Need total length maybe large. Use `pr = np.asarray(p)[::-1]`.
```
def cross_corr_lag(a, p):
    n = len(a); L = len(p)
    total = n + L - 1
    fs = _next_pow2(total)
    A = rfft(a, fs)
    P = rfft(p[::-1], fs)
    conv = irfft(A*P, fs)[:total]
    if L == 1:
       return conv[:n]
    return conv[L-1 : n-2 : -1]
```
Check for stop when n=1: `n-2=-1`; stop -1 in negative? For slice start L-1, stop -1, step -1 yields down to 0? If n=1, want length1 index L-1. `conv[L-1 : -1 : -1]` with stop -1 (len-1) excludes -1, so maybe none? Better handle n==1 separately. n not 1 likely. Add if n==1: return conv[L-1:L] maybe.
```
if n==1: return conv[L-1:L+?]? Actually slice length1: conv[L-1:L]? step? Use np.array([conv[L-1]])
```

```
def newton_to_target(a0, target, time_limit, reg=1e-8, alphas=None, max_no_improve=40, verbose=False):
    start=time.monotonic()
    a,c = score_and_conv(a0)
    if not finite: return a0,a
    ...
    n=len(a); L=2*n-1; N=_next_pow2(L)
    best=a.copy(); best_score=sc
    target=np.asarray(target); target=np.maximum(target,0); ts=target.sum(); if ts>1e-12: target=target/ts
    alphas = alphas or (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125,0.0625,0.03125)
    P = rfft(a,N)
    no=0; it=0
    while time...:
       it+=1
       r = target - c
       R = rfft(r,N)
       absP2 = np.abs(P)**2
       denom = 4*absP2 + reg*np.max(absP2+1.0?)
```
Use `reg_scale = np.max(absP2) if np.max(absP2)>0 else 1.0`; `denom=4*absP2 + reg*reg_scale`. reg default 1e-6.
```
       d_hat = (2*np.conj(P)*R)/denom
       d = irfft(d_hat,N)[:n]
       if not finite d: d=zeros
       # Maybe scale step to units of a
       step_scale = np.max(np.abs(d))
       # If step huge, line search alpha smaller.
       found=False
       alpha= float(alphas[0])
       for _ in range(len(alphas)):
          cand = a + alpha*d
          cand = np.maximum(cand, 0.0)
          cand_s = cand.sum()
          if cand_s > 1e-14:
             cand/=cand_s
             scand,cand_c = score_and_conv(cand)
             if scand < best_score - 1e-14:
                a=cand; c=cand_c; best=a.copy(); best_score=scand; found=True; no=0; break
          alpha *= 0.5
       if not found:
          no +=1
          if no % 5 == 0:
             reg = max(1e-12, reg*0.5) if no < 30 else min(1e-2, reg*2.0)
          if no > max_no_improve: break
       P = rfft(a,N)
       if verbose and it%200==0: print...
    return best,best_score
```
The condition `scand < best_score` ensures global monotone. If candidate improves current but not best (current may be worse than best? We update best each accept, current=best always). Good.

`dual_improve`:
```
def dual_improve(a0, time_limit, beta_init=100.0, max_no_improve=60, verbose=False):
   a,c,score; best...
   beta=beta_init; no=0; it=0
   while time...:
      it++
      p=softmax_weights(c,beta)
      d=cross_corr_lag(a,p)
      ds=d.sum(); if ds>1e-14: b=d/ds else b=a
      # blend to avoid zeros
      found=False
      for alpha in (1.0,0.7,0.5,0.3,0.15):
         cand=(1-alpha)*a + alpha*b
         scand,cand_c=score_and_conv(cand)
         if scand < best_score-1e-14:
            a=cand; c=cand_c; best=...; found=True; no=0; break
      if found:
         # maybe adjust beta slightly
      else:
         no+=1
         if no % 10 ==0:
            beta *= 1.5
            if beta > 10000: beta=10.0
         if no>max_no_improve: break
   return best,best_score
```
Dual update from p weights over all lags. If p uniform near, may move toward something. Good.

def run_lgbfgs(a0, time_limit, betas=(10.0,50.0,200.0,1000.0,5000.0), maxfun_per_beta=1000, verbose=False):
   from scipy.optimize import minimize
   start=time.monotonic(); a,best...; n=len(a)
   pos=np.maximum(a,1e-300); logs=np.log(pos); med=np.median(logs); j0=int(np.argmin(np.abs(logs-med)))
   t=np.empty(n); t=logs-logs[j0]; t=np.clip(t,-35,35); t[j0]=0
   bounds=[(-35,35)]*n; bounds[j0]=(0,0)
   for beta in betas:
      rem=time_limit - (time.monotonic()-start); if rem<30 break
      mf=min(maxfun_per_beta, max(100, int(rem*10))) # heuristic
      res=minimize(smooth_score_grad, t, args=(n,j0,float(beta)), method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, bounds=bounds, options={'maxfun':mf, 'maxiter':200, 'ftol':1e-14, 'gtol':1e-10, 'maxls':50})
      if hasattr(res,'x') and np.all(np.isfinite(res.x)):
         t_new=res.x.copy(); t_new[j0]=0
         e=np.exp(t_new - t_new.max()); a_new=e/e.sum(); sc,cand_c=score_and_conv(a_new)
         if sc < best_score: best=a_new; best_score=sc
         t=t_new
   return best,best_score
```
The `maxfun` heuristic maybe too large. Use `min(2000, max(80, int(rem/2)))`? If rem=200s, int(100). Maybe okay. Let's set `mf = min(1500, max(80, int(rem/3)))`. Not critical.

`coordinate_descent` details:
```
def coordinate_descent(a0, time_limit, active_tol=0.05, verbose=False):
   a,c,score=score_and_conv(a0); best=a; best_score=score; n=len(a); L=2n-1
   factors_large=[0.95,0.98,0.99,1.01,1.02,1.05]
   factors_small=[0.985,0.995,1.005,1.015]
   use_small=False
   no_improve=0
   start=time.monotonic()
   while time...:
      M=float(c.max()); S=1.0
      # active mask
      thresh = M*(1.0-active_tol)
      if not use_small: active_lags=np.where(c >= thresh)[0]
      else: active_lags=np.where(c >= M*(1.0-0.005))[0]
      if len(active_lags)==0: active_lags=[int(np.argmax(c))]
      active=np.zeros(n,bool)
      for k in active_lags:
         i0=max(0,k-n+1); i1=min(n-1,k); active[i0:i1+1]=True
      coords=np.nonzero(active)[0]
      if len(coords)==0: coords=np.arange(n)
      improved=False
      factors = factors_small if use_small else factors_large
      for i in coords:
         old=a[i]
         if old < 1e-16: continue
         k0=i; k1=min(i+n-1, L-1)
         left = float(c[:k0].max()) if k0>0 else -1.0
         right = float(c[k1+1:].max()) if k1+1<L else -1.0
         unaff = left if left>right else right
         base = c[k0:k1+1]
         # if old small? affected
         best_factor=None; best_y=old; best_newM=M; best_score_local=best_score
         for f in factors:
             y=old*f
             delta=y-old
             S_new=S+delta
             if S_new < 1e-12: continue
             affected = base + 2.0*delta*a
             idx2=2*i - k0
             if 0 <= idx2 < affected.size:
                 affected[idx2] = c[2*i] + 2.0*old*delta + delta*delta
             nm = float(affected.max())
             if unaff > nm: nm=unaff
             sc_local = 2.0*n*nm/(S_new*S_new)
             if sc_local < best_score_local - 1e-15:
                best_score_local=sc_local; best_newM=nm; best_factor=f; best_y=y
         if best_factor is not None:
             delta=best_y-old
             changes=2.0*delta*a
             idx2=2*i-k0
             if 0<=idx2<changes.size:
                changes[idx2]=2.0*old*delta + delta*delta
             c[k0:k1+1] += changes
             a[i]=best_y
             S += delta
             M=best_newM
             improved=True
      # end for coords
      if improved:
          no_improve=0
      else:
          no_improve+=1
          if no_improve ==1: use_small=True
          if no_improve > 4: break
      # recompute normalized
      sc_cur,c_cur=score_and_conv(a)
      if np.isfinite(sc_cur) and sc_cur < best_score - 1e-14:
          best_score=sc_cur; best=a.copy()
      a=a.copy(); c=c_cur; # c_cur normalized conv
      if verbose and sweep%50==0: print
   return best,best_score
```
But note after updates, `S` may not be 1; at end `score_and_conv` normalizes. The local score comparisons within sweep used unnormalized score. It is monotone relative to previous current unnormalized? We set `best_score_local=best_score` (normalized maybe from previous sweep). But current unnormalized score at start of sweep might equal best_score. After each accepted update, `sc_local` uses S_new and should be <= previous current score. Is it always <= `best_score_local`? We compare to `best_score` global, not current score. At start, current score = best_score (if we updated best previous). If an update improves current score to sc_local, it will be < best_score, accepted. Good. If global best not updated due to previous sweep not improving, current score might equal best_score. Good. If current score is worse than best_score? We always recompute and if not improve keep a? In code, if sc_cur not < best_score, we still set a normalized? If sc_cur worse, we would regress. We should not regress. Keep best separately and current maybe worse. In loop, start current is normalized best? We can ensure current a is always best. def propose_candidate():
    start=time.monotonic()
    hard_start=start
    deadline = start + 1780.0
    best_seq = None
    best_score = np.inf
    # update helper? cannot nested. Use code inline each time.
    # load parent
    try:
       p_arr = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
       p_arr = np.clip(p_arr,0.0,1000.0)
       if p_arr.ndim==1 and p_arr.size>0 and np.all(np.isfinite(p_arr)) and p_arr.sum()>0.01:
          p_score, p_c = score_and_conv(p_arr)

           bases.append(interpolate_seq(best_seq,n))
        bases.append(init_fourier(n, rng))
```

```python
"""
Search for non-negative step-function heights minimizing
    2 * n * max(conv(a, a)) / (sum(a)^2)

The algorithm combines several complementary ideas:
  * A globally useful Gauss-Newton step trying to make conv(a,a) as flat as
    possible (target is the uniform sequence of the right length).
  * A dual best-response / multiplicative update derived from active peaks.
  * Smooth max L-BFGS polishing in log-softmax coordinates.
  * Exact coordinate descent for final non-smooth max polishing.
  * Multi-length exploration and interpolation.

The function propose_candidate() respects a hard wall-clock budget and returns
the best sequence found.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

from scipy.fft import rfft, irfft
from operator import itemgetter


def _next_pow2(m):
    m = int(m)
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def fast_convolve(a):
    """Linear convolution of a with itself using FFT."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    size = 2 * n - 1
    fs = _next_pow2(size)
    A = rfft(a, fs)
    c = irfft(A * A, fs)[:size]
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    c[np.abs(c) < 1e-18] = 0.0
    return c


def score_and_conv(a):
    """Return (score, normalized convolution) for a non-negative sequence."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 1e-14 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.inf, None
    a = a / s
    n = len(a)
    c = fast_convolve(a)
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(c)):
        return np.inf, None
    c = np.clip(c, 0.0, None)
    return float(2.0 * n * c.max()), c


def make_forward_unnorm(n):
    """Unnormalized forward central-binomial profile."""
    a = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n <= 0:
        return a
    a[0] = 1.0
    for i in range(1, n):
        a[i] = a[i - 1] * (2.0 * i - 1.0) / (2.0 * i)
    return a


def make_forward(n):
    a = make_forward_unnorm(n)
    s = a.sum()
    if s < 1e-14:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n
    return a / s


def make_bumped(n, factor=0.25, width_frac=0.10):
    """Forward profile plus a smooth right-side bump."""
    f = make_forward_unnorm(n)
    if n <= 1:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / max(1, n)
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    width = max(1.0, float(width_frac) * n)
    bump = np.exp(-(n - 1.0 - x) / width)
    a = f + float(factor) * bump
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = a.sum()
    if s < 1e-14:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n
    return a / s


def interpolate_seq(f, new_n):
    """Linearly interpolate a normalized non-negative sequence to a new length."""
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64)
    f = np.clip(f, 0.0, None)
    s = f.sum()
    if s < 1e-14:
        return np.ones(new_n, dtype=np.float64) / new_n
    f = f / s
    old_n = len(f)
    if old_n == new_n:
        return f.copy()
    old_x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, old_n)
    new_x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, new_n)
    out = np.interp(new_x, old_x, f)
    out = np.maximum(out, 0.0)
    s2 = out.sum()
    if s2 < 1e-14:
        return np.ones(new_n, dtype=np.float64) / new_n
    return out / s2


def init_fourier(n, rng):
    """Random non-negative Fourier-superposition initializer."""
    n_h = int(rng.integers(3, 18))
    amps = rng.random(n_h) * 0.35
    phases = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi, n_h)
    freqs = np.arange(1, n_h + 1)
    t = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.full(n, 0.5, dtype=np.float64)
    for j in range(n_h):
        a += amps[j] * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * freqs[j] * t / n + phases[j])
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = a.sum()
    if s < 1e-14:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n
    return a / s


def uniform_target(n):
    """Uniform target of length 2n-1 with unit sum."""
    L = 2 * n - 1
    return np.full(L, 1.0 / L, dtype=np.float64)


def softmax_weights(c, beta):
    """Stable softmax weights over convolution coefficients."""
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    m = float(c.max())
    z = beta * (c - m)
    z = np.clip(z, -745.0, 0.0)
    ez = np.exp(z)
    s = float(ez.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(s):
        w = np.zeros(c.size, dtype=np.float64)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        return w
    return ez / s


def cross_corr_lag(a, p):
    """
    For a of length n and p of length L, return d_i = sum_j a_j p_{i+j}
    for i = 0,...,n-1, with p_t=0 outside its range.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = len(a)
    L = len(p)
    if n == 0 or L == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([float(p[0]) * float(a[0])], dtype=np.float64)
    total = n + L - 1
    fs = _next_pow2(total)
    A = rfft(a, fs)
    Pr = rfft(p[::-1], fs)
    conv = irfft(A * Pr, fs)[:total]
    conv = np.asarray(conv, dtype=np.float64)
    # d_i = conv[L-1-i]
    return conv[L - 1 : n - 2 : -1]


def newton_to_target(a0, target, time_limit, reg=1e-6, verbose=False):
    """
    Gauss-Newton style search for a*a ~= target, with exact-score line search.

    This is a powerful global step: it tries to make the convolution flat, then
    accepts only moves that truly reduce the exact objective.
    """
    start = time.monotonic()
    sc, c = score_and_conv(a0)
    if not np.isfinite(sc):
        return np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).copy(), sc

    a = np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    s = a.sum()
    if s > 0:
        a = a / s
    sc, c = score_and_conv(a)
    if not np.isfinite(sc):
        return a, np.inf

    n = len(a)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    N = _next_pow2(L)

    target = np.asarray(target, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    target = np.clip(target, 0.0, None)
    ts = target.sum()
    if ts < 1e-14 or not np.isfinite(ts):
        target = uniform_target(n)
    else:
        target = target / ts

    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = sc
    alphas = (1.0, 0.75, 0.5, 0.35, 0.2, 0.1, 0.05, 0.02)
    max_no_improve = 45
    no_imp = 0
    it = 0
    P = rfft(a, N)

    while (time.monotonic() - start) < time_limit:
        it += 1
        rem = time_limit - (time.monotonic() - start)
        if rem <= 0.1:
            break

        r = target - c
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(r)):
            break

        R = rfft(r, N)
        absP2 = np.abs(P) ** 2
        reg_scale = float(np.max(absP2))
        if not np.isfinite(reg_scale) or reg_scale < 1e-30:
            reg_scale = 1.0
        denom = 4.0 * absP2 + reg_scale * reg
        dhat = (2.0 * np.conj(P) * R) / denom
        d = irfft(dhat, N)[:n]
        d = np.asarray(d, dtype=np.float64)

        found = False
        alpha = alphas[0]
        for _ in range(len(alphas)):
            cand = a + alpha * d
            cand = np.maximum(cand, 0.0)
            ss = cand.sum()
            if ss > 1e-14 and np.isfinite(ss):
                cand = cand / ss
                csc, cc = score_and_conv(cand)
                if np.isfinite(csc) and csc < best_score - 1e-13:
                    a = cand
                    c = cc
                    best_a = a.copy()
                    best_score = csc
                    found = True
                    no_imp = 0
                    break
            alpha *= 0.5

        if not found:
            no_imp += 1

            # Fallback: dual best-response / multiplicative update.
            if no_imp > 8:
                pw = softmax_weights(c, 200.0)
                dd = cross_corr_lag(a, pw)
                ds = dd.sum()
                if ds > 1e-14 and np.isfinite(ds):
                    b = dd / ds
                    for beta in (1.0, 0.75, 0.5, 0.3, 0.15):
                        cand = (1.0 - beta) * a + beta * b
                        csc, cc = score_and_conv(cand)
                        if np.isfinite(csc) and csc < best_score - 1e-13:
                            a = cand
                            c = cc
                            best_a = a.copy()
                            best_score = csc
                            found = True
                            no_imp = 0
                            break

            if not found:
                if no_imp % 5 == 0:
                    if no_imp < 30:
                        reg = min(1e-2, reg * 2.0)
                    else:
                        reg = max(1e-9, reg * 0.5)
                if no_imp > max_no_improve:
                    break

        P = rfft(a, N)

        if verbose and it % 500 == 0:
            print(f"    Newton target: it={it} best={best_score:.9f}")

    return best_a, best_score


def dual_improve(a0, time_limit, beta_init=100.0, verbose=False):
    """
    Dual best-response iterations.

    Given a, choose weights on peaks of a*a, form the conjugate vector
    d_i = sum_k p_k a_{k-i}, and blend toward d. Acceptance is by exact score.
    """
    start = time.monotonic()
    sc, c = score_and_conv(a0)
    if not np.isfinite(sc):
        return np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).copy(), sc

    a = np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = sc
    beta = float(beta_init)
    no_imp = 0
    it = 0

    while (time.monotonic() - start) < time_limit:
        it += 1
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(c)):
            break

        p = softmax_weights(c, beta)
        d = cross_corr_lag(a, p)
        ds = d.sum()
        if ds <= 1e-14 or not np.isfinite(ds):
            d = a.copy()
            ds = d.sum()

        if ds > 1e-14:
            b = d / ds
        else:
            b = a.copy()

        found = False
        for alpha in (1.0, 0.75, 0.5, 0.3, 0.15, 0.075):
            cand = (1.0 - alpha) * a + alpha * b
            csc, cc = score_and_conv(cand)
            if np.isfinite(csc) and csc < best_score - 1e-13:
                a = cand
                c = cc
                best_a = a.copy()
                best_score = csc
                found = True
                no_imp = 0
                break

        if not found:
            no_imp += 1
            if no_imp % 10 == 0:
                beta *= 1.6
                if beta > 20000.0:
                    beta = 20.0
            if no_imp > 70:
                break

        if verbose and it % 1000 == 0:
            print(f"    Dual: it={it} best={best_score:.9f} beta={beta:.3f}")

    return best_a, best_score


def smooth_score_grad(v, n, j0, beta):
    """
    Objective and gradient for L-BFGS in log-softmax coordinates.

    Variables v define a_i = softmax(v_i), with v_{j0} fixed to 0 to remove the
    scale direction. Objective is a smoothed version of 2n max(conv(a,a)).
    """
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    t = v.copy()
    t[j0] = 0.0

    m = float(t.max())
    e = np.exp(t - m)
    s = e.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return 1e9, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    a = e / s
    c = fast_convolve(a)
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(c)):
        return 1e9, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    maxc = float(c.max())
    z = beta * (c - maxc)
    z = np.clip(z, -745.0, 0.0)
    ez = np.exp(z)
    ps = float(ez.sum())
    if ps <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(ps):
        p = np.zeros(c.size, dtype=np.float64)
        p[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        soft = maxc
        p = p / max(p.sum(), 1e-300)
    else:
        p = ez / ps
        soft = maxc + float(np.log(ps)) / beta

    d = cross_corr_lag(a, p)
    P = float(np.dot(p, c))
    if not np.isfinite(soft) or not np.all(np.isfinite(d)):
        return 1e9, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    # F = soft max. dF/da_i = 2 d_i.
    # With a = softmax(t), dt gradient = 2 a_i (d_i - P).
    gradF = 2.0 * a * (d - P)
    score = 2.0 * n * soft
    grad = 2.0 * n * gradF
    grad = np.asarray(grad, dtype=np.float64)
    grad[j0] = 0.0
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
        grad = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    return float(score), grad


def run_lgbfgs(a0, time_limit, betas=(10.0, 50.0, 200.0, 1000.0, 5000.0), verbose=False):
    """Smoothed-max L-BFGS polishing in log-softmax coordinates."""
    start = time.monotonic()
    try:
        from scipy.optimize import minimize
    except Exception:
        return np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).copy(), score_and_conv(a0)[0]

    sc, _ = score_and_conv(a0)
    if not np.isfinite(sc):
        return np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).copy(), np.inf

    n = len(a0)
    a = np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    s = a.sum()
    if s > 0:
        a = a / s

    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score, _ = score_and_conv(best_a)

    pos = np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
    logs = np.log(pos)
    med = float(np.median(logs))
    j0 = int(np.argmin(np.abs(logs - med)))

    t = logs - logs[j0]
    t = np.clip(t, -35.0, 35.0)
    t[j0] = 0.0

    bounds = [(-35.0, 35.0) for _ in range(n)]
    bounds[j0] = (0.0, 0.0)

    for beta in betas:
        rem = time_limit - (time.monotonic() - start)
        if rem < 30.0:
            break
        maxfun = int(min(1800, max(100, rem / 2.0)))
        try:
            res = minimize(
                smooth_score_grad,
                t.copy(),
                args=(n, j0, float(beta)),
                method="L-BFGS-B",
                jac=True,
                bounds=bounds,
                options={
                    "maxfun": maxfun,
                    "maxiter": 300,
                    "ftol": 1e-14,
                    "gtol": 1e-10,
                    "maxls": 50,
                },
            )
        except Exception:
            continue

        if hasattr(res, "x"):
            tx = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
            if tx.size == n and np.all(np.isfinite(tx)):
                tx = tx.copy()
                tx[j0] = 0.0
                m = float(tx.max())
                e = np.exp(tx - m)
                ss = e.sum()
                if ss > 1e-14 and np.isfinite(ss):
                    a_new = e / ss
                    sc_new, _ = score_and_conv(a_new)
                    if np.isfinite(sc_new):
                        if sc_new < best_score - 1e-13:
                            best_a = a_new.copy()
                            best_score = sc_new
                        # Keep the current iterate only if it is not much worse.
                        if sc_new <= best_score + 1e-8:
                            t = tx.copy()

        if verbose:
            print(f"    L-BFGS beta={beta:.1f} best={best_score:.9f}")

    return best_a, best_score


def coordinate_descent(a0, time_limit, verbose=False):
    """
    Exact coordinate descent on the nonsmooth objective.

    For each coordinate, tries a small geometric set of multipliers and accepts
    only if the exact objective decreases. Active coordinates are prioritized,
    then the method switches to full/small-factor mode if needed.
    """
    start = time.monotonic()
    sc, c = score_and_conv(a0)
    if not np.isfinite(sc):
        return np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).copy(), sc

    n = len(a0)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    a = np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = sc

    factors_large = (0.95, 0.98, 0.99, 0.995, 1.005, 1.01, 1.02, 1.05)
    factors_small = (0.99, 0.995, 1.005, 1.01)

    use_small = False
    active_tol = 0.05
    no_imp = 0
    sweep = 0

    while (time.monotonic() - start) < time_limit:
        rem = time_limit - (time.monotonic() - start)
        if rem < 2.0:
            break

        sweep += 1
        M = float(c.max())
        S = float(a.sum())
        if S <= 1e-14 or not np.isfinite(S):
            break

        thresh = M * (1.0 - active_tol)
        if not np.isfinite(thresh) or thresh < 0.0:
            thresh = 0.0
        active_lags = np.where(c >= thresh)[0]
        if active_lags.size == 0:
            active_lags = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))], dtype=int)

        active = np.zeros(n, dtype=bool)
        for k in active_lags:
            k = int(k)
            i0 = max(0, k - n + 1)
            i1 = min(n - 1, k)
            active[i0:i1 + 1] = True

        coords = np.nonzero(active)[0]
        if coords.size == 0:
            coords = np.arange(n)

        use_small_now = use_small
        factors = factors_small if use_small_now else factors_large
        improved = False

        for i in coords:
            i = int(i)
            old = float(a[i])
            if old < 1e-16:
                continue

            k0 = i
            k1 = i + n - 1
            if k1 > L - 1:
                k1 = L - 1

            left = float(c[:k0].max()) if k0 > 0 else -1.0
            right = float(c[k1 + 1:].max()) if (k1 + 1) < L else -1.0
            unaff = left if left > right else right

            base = c[k0:k1 + 1]

            best_factor = None
            best_y = old
            best_newM = M
            best_local_score = best_score

            for f in factors:
                y = old * f
                delta = y - old
                S_new = S + delta
                if S_new < 1e-12:
                    continue

                affected = base + 2.0 * delta * a
                # The quadratic term is at k = 2i, offset i in [k0:k1+1].
                idx2 = i
                if 0 <= idx2 < affected.size:
                    affected[idx2] = c[2 * i] + 2.0 * old * delta + delta * delta

                nm = float(affected.max())
                if unaff > nm:
                    nm = unaff

                sc_loc = float(2.0 * n * nm / (S_new * S_new))
                if np.isfinite(sc_loc) and sc_loc < best_local_score - 1e-15:
                    best_local_score = sc_loc
                    best_newM = nm
                    best_factor = f
                    best_y = y

            if best_factor is not None:
                delta = best_y - old
                changes = 2.0 * delta * a
                idx2 = i
                if 0 <= idx2 < changes.size:
                    changes[idx2] = 2.0 * old * delta + delta * delta
                c[k0:k1 + 1] += changes
                a[i] = best_y
                S += delta
                M = best_newM
                improved = True

        if improved:
            no_imp = 0
        else:
            no_imp += 1
            if no_imp == 1:
                use_small = True
            if no_imp >= 2:
                active_tol = 1.5
            if no_imp >= 3 and active_tol >= 1.0 and use_small:
                break

        sc_cur, c_cur = score_and_conv(a)
        if np.isfinite(sc_cur) and sc_cur < best_score - 1e-14:
            best_score = sc_cur
            best_a = a.copy()

        a = best_a.copy()
        c = c_cur if c_cur is not None else fast_convolve(a)
        c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
        c = np.clip(c, 0.0, None)

        if verbose and sweep % 25 == 0:
            print(f"    CD sweep={sweep} best={best_score:.9f} active_tol={active_tol:.3f}")

    return best_a, best_score


def propose_candidate():
    """
    Main search entry point.

    Returns Python list of non-negative heights.
    """
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t_start + 1780.0  # leave a safety margin before 1800s

    best_seq = None
    best_score = np.inf

    # Optional parent sequence from previous runs.
    try:
        p = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
        p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if p.ndim == 1 and p.size > 0 and np.all(np.isfinite(p)) and p.sum() > 0.01:
            ps, pc = score_and_conv(p)
            if np.isfinite(ps):
                best_seq = p / p.sum()
                best_score = ps
                print(f"[init] parent: n={len(best_seq)} score={best_score:.9f}")
    except Exception:
        pass

    # Build diverse starting sequences.
    bases = []
    if best_seq is not None:
        bases.append(best_seq.copy())

    n_list = []
    if best_seq is not None:
        nparent = len(best_seq)
        n_list.append(nparent)
        for x in (1024, 1536, 2048):
            if x not in n_list:
                n_list.append(x)
        if 2 * nparent <= 4096 and (2 * nparent) not in n_list:
            n_list.append(2 * nparent)
    else:
        n_list = [1024, 1536, 2048]

    rng = np.random.default_rng(20240501)

    for n in n_list:
        if n <= 0:
            continue
        bases.append(make_forward(n))
        bases.append(make_bumped(n, 0.15, 0.18))
        bases.append(make_bumped(n, 0.50, 0.06))
        if best_seq is not None:
            bases.append(interpolate_seq(best_seq, n))
        bases.append(init_fourier(n, rng))

    scored_bases = []
    for idx, b in enumerate(bases):
        b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
        if b.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(b)):
            continue
        bs, _ = score_and_conv(b)
        if np.isfinite(bs):
            if bs < best_score:
                s = b.sum()
                if s > 0:
                    best_seq = b / s
                else:
                    best_seq = b.copy()
                best_score = bs
            scored_bases.append((bs, idx, b))
            if len(scored_bases) <= 12 or bs < best_score + 1e-4:
                print(f"[base] n={b.size} score={bs:.9f}")

    if len(scored_bases) > 0:
        scored_bases.sort(key=itemgetter(0))

    # Phase 1: Newton-to-uniform from several diverse starts.
    for item in scored_bases[:8]:
        rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem < 90.0:
            break
        b = item[2]
        t_new = min(65.0, rem * 0.10)
        a_new, sc_new = newton_to_target(b, uniform_target(len(b)), t_new)
        if np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < best_score:
            best_seq = a_new.copy()
            best_score = sc_new
            print(f"[newton-base] n={len(best_seq)} score={best_score:.9f}")

    # Phase 2: Extended Newton from current best.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if best_seq is not None and rem > 260.0:
        t_ext = min(320.0, rem * 0.38)
        a_new, sc_new = newton_to_target(best_seq, uniform_target(len(best_seq)), t_ext)
        if np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < best_score:
            best_seq = a_new.copy()
            best_score = sc_new
            print(f"[newton-ext] n={len(best_seq)} score={best_score:.9f}")

    # Phase 3: Dual best-response.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if best_seq is not None and rem > 200.0:
        t_dual = min(170.0, rem * 0.28)
        a_new, sc_new = dual_improve(best_seq, t_dual)
        if np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < best_score:
            best_seq = a_new.copy()
            best_score = sc_new
            print(f"[dual] n={len(best_seq)} score={best_score:.9f}")

    # Phase 4: Smooth L-BFGS polishing.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if best_seq is not None and len(best_seq) <= 4096 and rem > 320.0:
        t_lgb = min(330.0, rem * 0.40)
        a_new, sc_new = run_lgbfgs(best_seq, t_lgb, betas=(10.0, 50.0, 200.0, 1000.0, 5000.0))
        if np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < best_score:
            best_seq = a_new.copy()
            best_score = sc_new
            print(f"[lgbfgs] n={len(best_seq)} score={best_score:.9f}")

    # Phase 5: Exact coordinate descent.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if best_seq is not None and rem > 240.0:
        t_cd = min(480.0, rem * 0.46)
        a_new, sc_new = coordinate_descent(best_seq, t_cd)
        if np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < best_score:
            best_seq = a_new.copy()
            best_score = sc_new
            print(f"[cd1] n={len(best_seq)} score={best_score:.9f}")

    # Phase 6: Another Newton round after CD.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if best_seq is not None and rem > 200.0:
        t_ne = min(180.0, rem * 0.30)
        a_new, sc_new = newton_to_target(best_seq, uniform_target(len(best_seq)), t_ne)
        if np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < best_score:
            best_seq = a_new.copy()
            best_score = sc_new
            print(f"[newton2] n={len(best_seq)} score={best_score:.9f}")

    # Phase 7: Wrap up with coordinate descent.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if best_seq is not None and rem > 160.0:
        t_cd2 = min(200.0, rem * 0.45)
        a_new, sc_new = coordinate_descent(best_seq, t_cd2)
        if np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < best_score:
            best_seq = a_new.copy()
            best_score = sc_new
            print(f"[cd2] n={len(best_seq)} score={best_score:.9f}")

    # Phase 8: Explore a larger length from the best sequence if time remains.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if best_seq is not None and rem > 520.0:
        nb = len(best_seq)
        alt_n = 2048 if nb < 2048 else 4096
        if alt_n <= 5120 and alt_n != nb:
            init_alt = interpolate_seq(best_seq, alt_n)
            t1 = min(230.0, rem * 0.34)
            a_alt, sc_alt = newton_to_target(init_alt, uniform_target(alt_n), t1)
            if np.isfinite(sc_alt) and sc_alt < best_score:
                best_seq = a_alt.copy()
                best_score = sc_alt
                print(f"[larger-newton] n={nb}->{alt_n} score={best_score:.9f}")

            rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
            if rem > 260.0 and len(best_seq) <= 4096:
                t2 = min(170.0, rem * 0.36)
                a_alt2, sc_alt2 = run_lgbfgs(best_seq, t2, betas=(10.0, 100.0, 1000.0, 5000.0))
                if np.isfinite(sc_alt2) and sc_alt2 < best_score:
                    best_seq = a_alt2.copy()
                    best_score = sc_alt2
                    print(f"[larger-lgb] n={len(best_seq)} score={best_score:.9f}")

            rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
            if rem > 180.0:
                t3 = min(180.0, rem * 0.45)
                a_alt3, sc_alt3 = coordinate_descent(best_seq, t3)
                if np.isfinite(sc_alt3) and sc_alt3 < best_score:
                    best_seq = a_alt3.copy()
                    best_score = sc_alt3
                    print(f"[larger-cd] n={len(best_seq)} score={best_score:.9f}")

    # Fallback if nothing worked.
    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = make_forward(512)
        best_score, _ = score_and_conv(best_seq)

    # Final sanitization and official verification.
    best_seq = np.asarray(best_seq, dtype=np.float64)
    best_seq = np.clip(best_seq, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(best_seq.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-12:
        best_seq = make_forward(512)
        s = float(best_seq.sum())
    else:
        best_seq = best_seq / s

    try:
        official = float(evaluate_sequence(best_seq.tolist()))
        if np.isfinite(official):
            best_score = official
    except Exception:
        pass

    if not np.all(np.isfinite(best_seq)) or best_seq.size == 0:
        best_seq = make_forward(512)
        try:
            best_score = float(evaluate_sequence(best_seq.tolist()))
        except Exception:
            best_score = np.nan

    print(f"FINAL: n={len(best_seq)} score={best_score:.12f}")
    return [float(x) for x in best_seq]
```
